ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u5	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! I will briefly try to justify why the government changes regulations and introduces new ones. Ladies and gentlemen! In the beginning, however, I would like to explain that the aim of the bill of 2 June submitted for public consultation is not to limit the development of photovoltaics, on the contrary, it is to stimulate this industry, i.e. the photovoltaic industry, to make it even more developed. Photovoltaics, as you said, is already one of the most dynamically developing technologies, and many new jobs are being created. And that's exactly what we want, that's what we want to take care of. The total installed capacity at the end of April 2021 was 4,7 GW. And it is also worth noting here that in 2015 we had only 4 000. Prosumers and installed capacity were only 100 MW. Ladies and gentlemen! 3/4 of the power installed in photovoltaics are prosumers. The growing prosumer market today does not count 500, but already 600,000. entities. This spectacular success was achieved thanks to the following factors: falling costs of photovoltaics, my current programme, tax concessions, rising energy prices and waste systems. Observing the dynamic development of this sector, we see that it is necessary to modify the prosumer clearing system in order to ensure further stable development of this area. This is why legislative changes have been proposed in the Ministry of Climate and Environment. The draft amendment of the Act - Energy Law and the Renewable Energy Sources Act is the implementation of our commitments to Directive 2019/944 of the European Parliament and of the European Council of 5 June 2019 on common rules for the internal market in electricity and amending Directive 2012/27 of the European Union. Ladies and gentlemen! On 2 June, this legislative proposal was submitted for public and inter-ministerial consultations. We proposed, among others. a solution necessary to improve the functioning of the energy market, including strengthening the position of consumers and their activation. Consumers and active consumers, including prosumers, play a key role in achieving the flexibility needed to adapt the distributed electricity system. Without the flexibility provided by energy consumers, the transformation of the energy sector is, seems, impossible. This active role will be ensured by strengthening the position of consumers precisely and that is what we are also aiming for by proposing this law. They should therefore be provided with appropriate tools. So we're generally looking forward here - we're looking at what's going on today and how this prosumer market is going to look like in a moment. In this way, they will be able to adapt their energy consumption to the market situation, to those price signals in real time. The project developed in the Ministry of Climate and Environment provides the basis for such development, here we speak, among others. on the development of dynamic tariffs. In addition, EU regulations introduce an obligation to ensure that active customers, including prosumers, are among others. the rights to sell electricity on their own, will bear network charges reflecting costs and will be settled separately in terms of energy entering the grid and downloading from the network. Currently functioning in Poland, the discharge mechanism does not allow the separation of the settlement entered into the network and collected from the electricity grid. Ladies and gentlemen! What, then, are changes, what is the new system? The main prerequisite for new solutions for prosumer energy is to replace the system of waste with innovative solutions for new prosumers, thanks to which they will receive among others. the possibility of selling surplus electricity. And here the purchase price of the electricity prosumer will be 100%. the average price of electricity sales on the competitive market in the previous quarter announced by the President of URE. In order to meet the needs of prosumers, in the new planning system it is possible to sell the generated energy indefinitely. In order to ensure legal certainty for current prosumers, the proposed regulations will only apply to prosumers entering the market after 1 January 2022. Moreover, what is proposed in the Act does not prejudge the date of entry into force of the Act. Ladies and gentlemen, with a brief view to the summary, the amendment proposed on 2 June (The Bell) was submitted to a broad inter-ministerial and social consultation. The dialogue with the PV industry is ongoing, as demonstrated by the consultation meeting on 22 June, in which several dozen PV players took part. The opportunity to discuss and listen to the voices coming directly from the industry gives us the basis for building the solid foundations of this law. And she in the circles of manufacturers and aggregations is actually very positive. Ladies and gentlemen, I will sum up so briefly what is to change and what is to be the amendment of the law. We provide a prosumer perspective for at least 25 years. Today is only 15 years old. We provide rights acquired for current prosumers, i.e. we ensure the development and continuation of our activities. We provide a system that generates large savings on accounts, we limit spending unnecessary costs on an oversized installation, in which very often prosumers invest unnecessarily. We're developing an energy storage market. We build solid foundations for a new energy model based on distributed entities. To sum up, the model developed is the answer to the development of photovoltaics, not, as is alleged, the killing of this sector. In the long term, it is very forward-looking, we are running very forward-looking in time here and we see how this photovoltaics are developing in neighbouring countries, and we are already adapting to the fact that it is a practical, functional system that is cost-effective for prosumers. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u39	Mr. Marshal! Membership! I think that the signing of a social agreement with the social side in May this year is a historic event, because it is not only an event that ends the many months of talks that took place in a permanent dialogue with the social side, both in plenary meetings and in expert groups, regarding public aid or so-called. clean coal technologies, but also in companies themselves, in five mining groups there were teams dealing with social, aid and social security issues for workers and the decommissioning of individuals themselves. At the end of the day agreements were drawn up, which became part of the social contract together with the annexes, a comprehensive social agreement which regulates the liquidation of mining in Poland until 2049. This liquidation is based on a socially agreed timetable, but also on public aid instruments that should be mobilised in this process. These are instruments that miners already know, because it is primarily an instrument of extraordinary costs, costs that are necessary to liquidate assets to liquidate individual individuals, but also to ensure that in the case of workers there, they are provided with one-off cash checks or early retirement benefits. Such instruments are known in Polish mining. These instruments will also be in the new support system. An additional German-designed instrument is the subsidisation of capacity to reduce production. This capacity to reduce production is distributed over the years and will systematically produce coal in Poland. This is obviously due to the fact that the State will mobilise public aid in this way in order to contribute to these capacity reductions in production in order to ensure the financial stability of individual mines, individual companies. Companies covered by the support scheme are companies Tauron Extraction, Węglokox Country and the Polish Górnicza Group. Lubelski Węgiel Bogdanka and Jastrzębska Węglowa are not covered by this support system today and are not part of the application submitted to the European Commission for public aid. These are companies which operate on the stock exchange, market companies which conduct their business on market terms, producing and producing energy coal, and, above all, in the case of Jastrzębska Węglowa, coke coal, which today concerns an important part of European policy as a critical element for industry. If there is a situation that would require a change of proposal and joining the system also the entities that are in these companies, we will discuss this with the social side. This is one of the contract records. Of course, the agreement itself does not only cover the liquidation of the hard coal mining sector in Poland and all the aid instruments associated with it, but also shows the future of the mining areas, mainly Silesia, indicates the directions of development, and possible support instruments, and new solutions that will be proposed by the social side, such as the Silesian transformation fund and investment projects, e.g. a car factory in Jaworzno, which we want to carry out together. The project, i.e. the pipeline, the entire route from the Norwegian shelf through Denmark, under the Baltic Sea, with the connection to the Polish transmission system - the entire route will be completed on 1 October 2022, i.e. next year. Extensive work is being carried out on all sections of this route at the moment. The submarine pipeline, which connects the Norwegian transport system on the Norwegian shelf with Denmark, is already in place. In Denmark - it is a section of 210 km - works are carried out in many places. An investor, the operator of the Danish transmission system Energinet, is responsible for the work and construction in Denmark. This is the equivalent of our Gaz-System operator in Poland. The submarine stretch at the bottom of the Baltic Sea will begin to be arranged soon, within a few weeks. The schedule predicts that in the autumn of this year this section of 275 km will be arranged. This does not, of course, mean that he will already be active, because work is still needed to check it, test it, fill it with gas. In a word: it will be part of the entire route next year. Three new works are built in Poland, and these works are also done as planned. The pipes that are purchased and prepared for laying at the bottom of the Baltic are already at the disposal of the investor are ready. With regard to this problem, which you mentioned in the question, namely the problem with the environmental authorities and the administration of the environment in Denmark, this was as follows: On 31 May, the Danish Environment and Food Appeals Board withdrew the environmental permit issued to Energnet in 2019. It is about the habitat of mice and bats, which have been identified on the route already built - at the moment this construction is very advanced - the pipeline. As a result of the discussions and explanations provided by the Danish environmental administration and the agreements concluded, Energinet confirmed the deadline for completing this section in Denmark next year, as expected. Work has been renewed, and they are in progress. The Danish Environment Agency has authorised the renewal of the work carried out by Energinet in all areas where these habitats of mice and bats have not been identified. For these areas, a supplementary permit will be issued in the near future, but this will not affect the entire schedule for the investment. So, in conclusion, I would like to say that the strategic project we are conducting will lead to the fact that by the end of next year, 2022, we will have the opportunity to transport gas to Poland from the Norwegian shelf through the Baltic Pipe pipeline, as envisaged. The Jamaican contract, which expires at the end of next year, 2022, will be replaced by PGNiG, who is responsible for this, deliveries from the Norwegian shelf and from the terminal in Świnoujście, which we are also expanding and which will be ready for this, and today is already operational. ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u69	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Indeed, the plan for rare diseases is an area, as the Member himself pointed out, in which we have a gap to fill. It is worth pointing out that the area of rare diseases, even at the levels of the European Union, at the level of the European Parliament, the European Council, has been recognised a long time ago. Not only has it been noticed - the Member States have in some way been obliged... a Council Recommendation of 8 June 2009 on the development of a plan or strategy for rare diseases has been issued. As you can see, it's been 12 years and we haven't seen this plan. Now we're finalizing this work. The hard work, because the plan is multi-aread, and these assumptions that we want to dedicate in this area concern very difficult areas. The Member mentioned that there are several thousand sick units. They concern in total small groups, narrow in the case of a single disease unit, but in general we say that we can have 2 and 3 million people with a rare disease in Poland. It's a large group of people who are very difficult to diagnose, because it's really hard for doctors, whether at the basic level or even at the specialist level, to pick it up at the diagnostic level and qualify the patient as a suitable disease group. It is also difficult to treat, because therapeutic methods for rare diseases are obviously limited. Therefore, such a plan is needed - a plan that will tell us how to improve the situation in the diagnostic area, how to improve the situation in the therapeutic area, how to improve the situation in the area of communication with the patient. We started working on this plan or on it, let's say, operating at a somewhat unlucky moment, because right before the pandemic. We set up a team, if I remember correctly, in March or April of that year. It was a difficult time, but the band worked intensively despite the pandemic. We have been able to work out the plan's assumptions, we have been able to define specific tasks, we have been able to define specific responsibilities, we have worked out specific budgets for specific tasks. This plan was submitted for pre-consultation at the beginning of this year, later, in April 2021, included in the list of government work and 28 April, i.e. 2 months ago, was submitted for public consultation and inter-ministerial arrangements. The deadline for submitting comments was 21 May 2021. Since then, unfortunately, even though it's been a month, we've been analyzing these comments. The comments are substantive and relate to difficult areas, including the drug area. We know that access to drug therapy in the area of rare diseases is difficult, the results of clinical trials are often residual, scientific evidence is very limited, and therefore the procedures for assessing these drugs before the refund is implemented are complicated. We are proposing some simplifications, but they obviously raised doubts as to whether they required clarification by experts, certain solutions which were put forward in the course of submitting comments primarily by, and it is worth mentioning, patient organisations, patient associations. They have been involved in drawing up these comments, have had a huge impact on this, and in these areas they would like to clarify some more. I believe that really soon - the next time I define it as no longer than a month - this project will be handed over to the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers and the Council of Ministers and that the holiday will be the moment when the Council of Ministers will adopt a plan for rare diseases. It is worth mentioning, since I have 2 minutes, that the plan for rare diseases is not just a task, as I said, these are not specific areas. There are six areas. Maybe I'll start with that. It's very important. Rare disease expert centres. We want to designate these centres, show their references, also show patients where centres can count on diagnostics and comprehensive treatment. These centres are to meet the highest standards. Today, as Poland, we already have dozens of centres for the treatment of rare diseases qualified for European networks, qualified for the highest level of treatment. These are often pediatric centres for which rare diseases pose a significant challenge. The second area of this plan - I don't know if it's the most important - is the diagnosis of rare diseases. Today 80% rare diseases are genetic in nature. Diagnostic methods have progressed enormously in recent years. We need to keep up with this progress at the level of infrastructure support - such tasks are foreseen in the plan - and at the level of qualifying these benefits to the guaranteed benefits basket to enable individuals to settle them. The next areas are access to the medicines and special nutritional measures I mentioned and, above all, to facilitate the implementation procedures for refunds. There is an area of the rare disease registry and there are two areas that cared very much for patients, i.e. the information platform and the associated patient passport, i.e. de facto it is the provision of one reliable information channel for patients. They will be able to go from the level of diagnosis to the treatment, see which centres they specialize in, learn more about rare diseases. It is also an information portal for POZ doctors, for specialists who may not be dealing with rare diseases every day as such an educational portal. The plan isn't just about the tasks, it's about the money, the resources that follow it. We estimate that the size of the budget of this plan is about 130 million PLN, but these are not all the expenses we want to spend on rare diseases, it is very important. These are expenses that literally involve certain tasks in the plan. On the other hand, he called the Medical Fund, and there are also guaranteed measures, in particular for innovative drug therapies in rare diseases, and there these mechanisms are applied, plus, of course, the current measures of the National Health Fund. When we talk about extending the procedures that are feasible, we assume that in the area of genetic diagnosis the National Health Fund will invest between PLN 30 million and PLN 50 million annually, you can say.

Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health Sławomir Gadomski: Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! Indeed, the 40+-prophylaxis programme has become a reality. But maybe a little history, too. This programme, on the one hand, is obviously a response to the post-pandemic challenges, is a response to a certain health debt, say, which Poles after the pandemic have. On the other hand, I am looking at Minister Ratchurek-Żelazko here, with whom we worked on this programme earlier, even before the pandemic. In fact, it was a pandemic that in some ways prevented its effective launch earlier, because we had assumptions prepared more than a year ago. I think this program is perfect for today. Such a health check of Poles is extremely necessary also after these certain perturbations connected with access to the doctor, television, teleporade or not taking them, etc. I think that in recent days we have also shown other solutions, which are important for us in this area, for example, related to the POZ remuneration system or to the organizational standard in the field of teleporad. On 14 June of this year, a regulation of the Minister of Health on the pilot scheme was issued and the programme starts on 1 July, i.e. a de facto one week from now. Its primary objective is, of course, to include this group of increased risks, i.e. people aged 40 years and over, early preventive diagnostics, basic diagnosis, it needs to be made clear to itself, a basic diagnosis, which, of course, can later be implemented outside the programme under other programmes, for example preventive programmes. Like I said, people aged 40 and over are eligible for the program. It decides the calendar year, which is not the date of birth, it decides the calendar year. How do you get into the program? There are two channels of entry into the program, because the programme is generally related to diagnostic tests, while a criterion of entry is the completion of a preventive survey. It is important for us that this diagnostic package be preceded by a preventive survey, which will in some way assess the profile or risk of the patient. This survey will be filled out first of all, and this is the channel we care about most, through the patient's online account - it will appear automatically for all these patients - or by talking to a home care consultant, i.e. via the helpline. Then such a survey will be filled out by this consultant. Filling in the survey results in automatic posting of referrals. You don't have to go to any doctor, you don't have to pick up referrals. The referral will automatically be issued through the IKP and will be available in connection with the operation of e-direction for all providers who will implement this service. Providers, this is very important, are not just entities that today have a contract with the National Health Fund. The programme may also be submitted by those providers who do not currently have contracts with the NFZ. Of course, they must meet certain organisational conditions: hiring a doctor, a nurse, equipment specified in the regulation, possession of a point of collection of diagnostic materials, certain sanitary conditions, etc. If they meet the conditions, they can report to the National Health Fund. He's gonna sign a deal with them. He asked what patients could count on and what is this study package. As I said, these are basic tests: complete blood count, total cholesterol or lipid control panel, blood glucose, creatinine, urinary general examination, occult blood in faeces - an important study for us from the point of view of early diagnosis of colon cancer. In addition, the package for men will be extended to PSA, a prostate specific antigen, or prostate cancer prophylaxis. In addition, there will be such basic tests as: measurement of pressure, body weight, growth of the circumference in the belt, cardiac rhythm titration. It's also to make sure there's this contact with the medical staff. I firmly believe that, as part of this research, there will probably still be an additional interview so that medical staff will also encourage other preventive examinations. After all, we have programs in mammography, cervical cancer, cytology, colonoscopic colon cancer. This will also be the moment, in fact, that, I hope, this 40+ staff will interest patients in these additional studies. Of course, a certain result, a simplified analysis of the results of the study will also be available to patients via the patient's online account. If there are some disturbing symptoms there, then the next path will be an incentive to use the services of a primary healthcare doctor and then possibly to clarify what path, whether further diagnostic or therapeutic, should be implemented. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u85	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It is worth noting that the Government of the United Right has been seeking to improve the functioning of national parks since 2015 and improve the financial situation of these entities, including in particular the increase in salaries of employees of national parks. Such actions are possible, among others. by increasing the wage limit and increasing the amount of the budget subsidy. And here it is worth noting that during the years 2008, 2014, i.e. during the reign of our predecessors from the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party, the salary limit of the employees of national parks was not increased, which made it impossible to increase salaries. The same was true of the budget subsidies allocated to the current national parks, including the years 2012 and 2014, since previously the parks were fully subsidised from the state budget. This is not enough, it is worth pointing out that in 2014 the budget grant for parks was reduced by PLN 1 million. On the other hand, at the time when the Law and Justice embraced governments, already in 2016 the grant for parks increased by almost PLN 4.5 million, while at the same time increasing the total salary limit for employees of national parks by more than 10%. A further increase in both the budget subsidy and the salary limit for park workers took place in 2017. The grant for parks intended to increase the salary of park workers increased by PLN 1 million, while the salary limit of each park's employees increased by another 10%. In addition, in 2019 the remuneration limit for park workers was increased by an average of almost 4%. In 2020 the budget subsidy for parks was again increased by more than 800,000. zł, again the salary limit of each national park has been increased by 10%. In addition, due to the difficult covid situation and the loss of parks caused by the pandemic park, over PLN 5 million of special subsidies were received from the state budget. This shows, if we summarize the data for the period from 2015, that the target grant of the state budget for the current national parks increased by more than PLN 6 million. The wage limit has increased by more than 30%. The average salary of employees increased by about 800 PLN. This shows that the Government of the United Right is not only making every effort to improve the working conditions of national parks, but also doing so effectively. This, of course, is still not enough, of course it is not enough, but I stress that we are catching up on previous years. It is also worth pointing out that national parks used and can still use the forest fund to carry out their tasks in the years 2015 and 2020. The annual total amount for this purpose was PLN 35.70 million. Work is currently underway to prepare a draft law on national parks. This project responds to the identified challenges and needs that national park workers are currently discussing. We want to aggregate in a single legal act the provisions relating to the functioning of national parks both as forms of nature conservation and as organisational and legal entities. It is also worth noting that our highest form of nature conservation, which we all pride and praise, has not yet had a dedicated law. She used entries in other legal acts, mainly in the Nature Conservation Act. Moreover, in more than a hundred legal acts, the theme of national parks was regulated to a lesser or greater extent. In this context, this law is also intended to set out purely formal legal issues. The aim of this regulation is to fully standardise the activities of parks as institutional entities managing this highest form of nature conservation. Therefore, this act will define the tasks and principles of the activities of the parks, but also their organisation. It will contain legal standards for improving supervision of the functioning of national parks. We plan to create a new entity - Polish National Parks as a coherent unit. They will include the management of Polish National Parks and all national parks. As a result, Polish parks will become a state legal entity. There will be some kind of centralisation in the structure, which will give the opportunity not only to improve decision-making in substantive or administrative matters, but also financial flows between those parks which are in a better financial position and those which are in a worse financial situation. Not much, it is this management that will be able to comprehensively promote national parks both in the country and in the world. Today, every park promotes itself, while we as a country do not have the full opportunity to boast abroad about how these parks function, how we protect nature and how we could really learn from us other countries, especially those who have already destroyed nature in their territory. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u196	We proceed with the examination of item 18 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry on the governmental draft law on alien species (prints No 1187 and 1253). I would like to ask Mrs Anna Paluch to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u197	Thank you very much. High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on the government draft law on alien species. The draft in print No 1187 was sent to the committee on 19 May 2021 for its first reading. The Environment Commission carried out its first reading of the project on 10 June, followed by detailed work on the project, the result of which is the printing report No 1253. High Izbo! The Act implements European Union law, i.e. Regulation No 1143/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council on preventive and remedial action with regard to the introduction and spread of invasive alien species and Commission Directive 2018/350 amending Directive 2001/18/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards the risk assessment of genetically modified organisms for the environment. This first Regulation 1143/2014 entered into force on 1 January 2015 and is an essential tool for implementing the EU Biodiversity Strategy. Its use is intended to reduce the negative impact of invasive alien species on endangered species, reduce the threat to nature, prevent environmental, economic and social damage estimated in the European Union to around EUR 12 billion per year. High Izbo! In Poland, the existing system of protection against alien species operates on the basis of the regulations contained in the article. 120 of the Nature Conservation Act of 16 April 2004 and of the Regulation of the Minister for the Environment of 9 September 2011 on the list of plants and animals of alien species which, in the event of release into the natural environment, may endanger native species or natural habitats. And let me remind you that the bodies implementing these provisions are the General Environment Director and Regional Environmental Directors. High Izbo! This draft law adapts this national system for the eradication of alien species described above to the European Union standards in terms of substance and organisation, while the additional obligations arising from the implementation of Regulation 1143/2014 will be carried out by those state institutions which have so far carried out obligations in an equivalent manner. These include: General Environment Director, Regional Environmental Directors, Veterinary Inspection, State Plant and Seed Protection Inspection and Customs and Scarb Service. The project introduces e.g. new definitions of concepts such as invasive alien species, remedial actions, elimination. Mostly - 16 definitions out of 25 - refer to the Regulation 1143/2014 which I have already mentioned many times. The project also organises the division of competences between the different authorities, defines the authorities involved in issuing permits and prohibitions, maintaining the Central Data Register on Invasive Alien Species, monitoring and management of invasive alien species, carrying out border controls. The project shall lay down sanctions for non-compliance with the obligations relating to the eradication of alien species and shall ensure that the principle is implemented: the polluter shall pay by means of regulations which concern the introduction of the IGO into the environment as a result of intentional or unintentional human interference. High Izbo! Lists of invasive alien species presenting a risk to the European Union, because we have two categories according to these regulations, i.e. alien species presenting a risk to the European Union and presenting a risk to Poland, so the lists of these first invasive alien species are contained in the three implementing regulations of the European Commission: from 13 July 2016 - there are 37 species, from 12 July 2017 - includes 12 species and from 25 July 2019 - covers 17 species. The list of invasive alien species presenting a threat to Poland will be laid down in a regulation of the Council of Ministers for which the authorisation will constitute an article. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 of the bill discussed here. High Izbo! During the committee's work on the project, 16 substantive amendments were made to this proposal. The Legislative Office of the Sejm also prepared its amendments. Both the substantive amendments proposed by the Legislative Bureau and the amendments of an editorial, linguistic and legislative nature have been introduced into the project. The project as a whole was adopted by 21 votes in favour, with one vote against and four abstentions. High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, please adopt the draft Foreign Species Act, which is contained in the report of the Committee on Printing No 1253. I didn't think your marshal would be so generous that he would grant me 15 minutes, and I prepared a speech for five minutes. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The Foreign Species Act extends and adapts national legislation to Regulation 1143/2014 of 22 October 2014. It regulates the competences of individual central administration bodies, local governments and citizens. This law has 60 articles collected in 12 chapters that describe individual sections. In addition to the general, final and amending provisions, chapters describing the Central Data Register on Invasive Alien Species, prohibitions and authorisations concerning alien species, the treatment of those species in the environment, remedial action, remedial action taken when those species are identified in the environment, and the treatment of species which may potentially pose a risk to the European Union. There are also penalty payments, administrative penalties and criminal provisions included in this Act. During the first reading and discussion, there were doubts, mainly from the Legislative Bureau. It turns out that eliminating this dangerous plant can be very costly and time-consuming. And it has to be done systematically, because it's a species that is reborn very quickly. The bill also introduces a central register of invasive alien species, an open register of IGO. In certain cases, authorisations to withdraw from the IGO statutory bans will be allowed. The bill provides that animals which are an invasive alien species may be placed in animal asylum. It is worth mentioning that the bill provides for, among others. the possibility of remedial action to prevent the spread of IGO plants and animals by the mayor, mayor or president of the city, but where the entity responsible for carrying them is not able to carry them out. However, amendments are necessary. In its current form, the bill allows for remedial action one year after receiving information from the Mayor, Mayor or Mayor of the City about the need to carry it out. ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u233	Thank you very much, Marshal. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour of presenting our position on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. This position coincides with my intentions, which I followed in preparing this resolution. We believe that it is important for the Sejm, apart from distinguishing, reminding, honoring the most important political events, to remember important social, cultural and sporting events. That was the intention of preparing this resolution. The rations behind the commemoration of the 100th anniversary of the International Poznań Fair are expressed in the text of the resolution, which I will now read. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the commemoration of 100. Anniversary of the International Poznań Fair. In 2021 the 100th anniversary of the establishment of the Poznań International Exhibition - the largest national exhibition centre, deserved for the promotion and development of trade in Poland and Europe. Industrial exhibitions organized in Poznań in the second half of the 19th and early of the 20th century became an inspiration for merchants and city authorities to establish a permanent trade fair institution. The idea of creating a Polish fair and exhibition event in Poznań was established in May 1917 at the Congress of the Association of Buyers' Associations. Ongoing World War I postponed these plans, but already in 1919, after Poland regained its independence, Poznań merchants and industrialists started preparing for the organization of the Polish exhibition event. It became the first Poznan Fair organized on 28 May - 5 June 1921. The event served to present the achievements of entrepreneurs and was an instrument for the integration of the whole country. The first director was Mieczysław Krósankiewicz. The idea of cyclical trade fair activity in Poznań, intended to show the possibilities of Polish industry, trade and agriculture, was developed in the 20th century. The next stages were the internationalization of the Fair in 1925, followed by the organisation of the Universal National Exhibition in 1929. Thanks to the 4.5 millionth audience from all over the world, it has contributed to the global promotion of the economic opportunities of reborn Poland, has spread the name: the Poznań International Fair around the world, and has also provided a modern, extensive exhibition infrastructure with Poznań Fair in the leading European organisers of such events. [[♪ The bell ♪]] After World War II, the Fair was reactivated, which for years of real socialism was the commercial window of Poland on the world, and their presence also affected the inhabitants of Poznań. They had a major impact on the outbreak of anti-communist protests called the Poznań Uprising in 1956 on 28 June 1956 and contributed to the dissemination of information about it beyond the borders of Poland. Currently, being part of the MTP Group, the Poznań International Fair is the largest organizer of trade fairs, conferences, cultural and sporting events in Central and Eastern Europe, which is visited every year by about one million visitors from all over the world. On the occasion of the 100th anniversary of the establishment of the Poznań International Fair, the Polish Sejm expresses its appreciation for the institutions, its creators and generations of employees who have served well the economic development of Poland. The Law and Justice Club will support the draft resolution, which I also encourage the entire High House. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u234	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mr Rafał Grupiński, the Civic Coalition.

Ladies and gentlemen, in order for this to be done, I would like to read the draft resolution, which was accepted by the committee: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the celebration of the 45th anniversary of Radom's June 1976. On June 25, 1976, a labor protest took place in Radom, Ursus and Płock, where thousands of people opposed the plans of the communist authorities to raise food prices and deteriorating living and working conditions. The protest has spread to many other cities. The most dramatic events occurred in Radom, where workers from dozens of factories, including others. from Metal Works, Radomskie Zakłady Przemysłowy Skórzanego 'Radoskini', as well as residents of the city gathered under the seat of the Provincial Committee of the Polish United Workers' Party and demanded the cancellation of increases in food prices. After a few hours, the workers' protest in Radom was brutally and bloody suppressed by MO and ZOMO units. Many participants in the strike were arrested and beaten. It was then that the Poles first heard about the so-called. health paths, i.e. troopers of officers who beat the prisoners between them. Many of the Radom workers paid for their arrest and two people died. In the following days, the participants of the June 1976 events were repressed by the communist authorities. There were prison sentences in court proceedings, and many workers lost their jobs. The punishing hand of the communist party deprived the'rebellioned' city of central investment, delaying its development for many years, and the inhabitants of Radom the authorities of the Polish People's Republic called 'warcholes', wanting to brand them in the eyes of the whole society. However, the protest has succeeded in bringing to an end the food price increases planned by the Central Committee of the PZPR. The events of June 1976 and subsequent repression (The Bell) against its participants have become an impulse to set up a Committee for the Defence of Workers and then the Human Rights and Citizens Movement - the first openly active opposition organizations in the communist bloc. Historians emphasize that without June 1976 there would be no August 1980 or the rise of 'Solidarity'. The events of June 1976 changed Radom permanently. The 'city with sentence', as after tragic events were called Radom, must have struggled with the persecutions of communist authorities for years. However, the Communists never managed to break the heroic spirit, courage, or pursuit of freedom of the people of Radom. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, bearing in mind the tragic events of June 1976, pays tribute to the heroic inhabitants of Radom, Ursus and Płock, and also expresses the highest respect for their courage and dedication. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland also expresses its respect and thanks to those who then decided to hurry with the help of the repressed and their families. Please, High Seym... ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u265	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Let me zoom in a little bit. The 11th session of the World Urban Forum will take place from 26 to 30 June 2022 in Katowice under the heading: We are changing cities for a better future. This will be the first edition of this event hosted by the country of Central and Eastern Europe. The World Urban Forum, which takes place every 2 years, is the flagship event of the UN agenda, the programme of united nations for human settlements and one of the most recognizable international meetings on the exchange of views and experiences on urban challenges. The Forum will help raise public awareness of the problems facing cities today. The World Urban Forum can not only publicise them, but also motivate decision-makers to take bold action to correct them. Main topics of the most important meetings during the forum, so-called. dialogues, among others. A fair and sustainable future for cities for all, a greener future for cities, urban mobility, innovation and technology, building resilience of cities, especially an important subject after a pandemic and in the context of climate change. The organization scale of the forum is comparable to COP24 in Katowice, which took place in 2018. In the case of this event a special law was passed, which significantly facilitated the organisation of COP24 in Poland. Similarly, in the case of the organisation of the World Urban Forum, the provisions currently in force are not sufficient to carry out and coordinate the tasks of the public administration and services associated with the organisation of such a large event as the Forum. The draft law sets out the tasks relating to a number of organisational aspects and the rules governing the implementation of these tasks by all actors involved in the preparation of the Forum. Among the most important are: the tasks of public administrations, including security and public order, the tasks of a team coordinating the organisation of the World Urban Forum, the principles of funding the organisation of the Forum. The Minister responsible for regional development will be responsible for a significant number of tasks related to the organisation of the Forum. The Minister responsible for Home Affairs will be responsible for ensuring and monitoring the activities of the services involved in the organisation of the Security and Public Order Forum in accordance with the proposed law. The Silesian Voivode will be responsible for preparing a comprehensive security plan during the event in Katowice and providing medical security for the forum. The organization of the forum will of course also involve the local government, the president of the city of Katowice. In order to finance the organisation of the event in Katowice, the funds of the state budget are intended primarily to be allocated, with the provisions proposed in the Act governing in detail what these funds may be spent on. The maximum expenditure limit from the State budget resulting from the Law will be 193 326 thousand. divided into 2 years, in 2021 - 68 420 thousand and in 2022 - 124 906 thousand. The financial resources will be used to cover, inter alia, the cost of renting the International Congress Centre and Sports and Sports Hall Spodek, the cost of the logistics operator, whose task will be to organize and operate the 11th session of the World Urban Forum together with accompanying events. The draft law excludes the application of the provisions of the Act of 11 September 2019 - Public Procurement Law, but only in relation to contracts whose value is lower than the Union thresholds within the meaning of that Law. Just a quick note. This point was the biggest discussion at the committee meeting, but those of you who know how such a party should be prepared, at what rate, as all this is happening, understand very well that the Public Procurement Law could paralyze the preparation within one year of this great undertaking, in which up to 25 thousand people can participate. people, representatives of countries around the world. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Often the decisions that will be taken are very urgent to be implemented. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I request that this law be adopted by the High Sejm.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u299	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The draft resolution of the Sejm on amending the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, contained in the sejm print no. 903, was submitted by a group of members of the club Lewis. The Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary and Immunity Committee considered this project and carried out its first reading on 28 May 2021. The aim of the proposal, as justified by the applicants, is to restore the proper functioning of the Sejm committees by enabling the chairmen of the committee to convene meetings in accordance with the appropriate sanitary standards laid down by the order of the Marshal of the Sejm. During the presentation of the project, the applicants stressed that this change was to be an expression of a return to normality, because the Coronavirus epidemic was fortunately weakened. We hope that no one else will return except those waves that have already been. A little bit of time and circumstances have changed since then, because now, as we have all noticed, committees are called without this mode to which this change applies, without the use of electronic means of communication that allow for distance communication. Following the committee's first reading, the committee introduced a number of amendments to the original draft resolution, and in the committee's report we actually have three important entries. The first amendment concerns the proposal for paragraph 1. Article 2 198j received a new sound. It would now read: Meeting of the committee or subcommittee in the procedure referred to in paragraph. 1 - that mouth right there. I quote 1 - the chairman of the committee is convened. On the date and agenda of the meeting of the committee or subcommittee as referred to in paragraph. 1, the chairman of the committee shall notify the Marshal of the Sejm, who may not agree to the sitting. The wording of paragraph so far. Article 2 198j is the following: Meeting of the committee or subcommittee under the procedure referred to in paragraph. 1, convenes the Marshal of the Sejm after consulting the chairman of the committee. So this is a change from Marshal of the Sejm to chairman of the committee. And that mouth. 1 deals with these particular situations that we have been dealing with recently, but they may also be other situations, because they are: (a) the introduction of a state of emergency, a state of natural disaster or martial law, or (b) the state of epidemic within the meaning of the Act of 5 December 2008 on the prevention and control of infectious infections and diseases in humans, the meeting of a committee or subcommittee may be carried out using electronic means of communication enabling distance communication. Under this procedure, if this change is accepted by the High Sejm, the chairman of the committee will convene meetings, and not, as now, the Marshal of the Sejm. Of course, if it is necessary for meetings to be convened using the possibility of distance communication. For now, all committee meetings are already held, as we know, under the so-called procedure. ordinary, that is, without the use of this way of communicating. The second change is to withdraw the mouth. Article 6 198j. This paragraph is currently read: Until the committee meeting as referred to in paragraph 1. Article 1 - just quoted - provisions of Article. Article 154 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 shall not apply. Art. Article 154 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm are as follows: At the meeting of the committee, representatives of professional and social organisations and experts of the committee and other persons may attend at the invitation of the bureau of the committee or its chairman. If this paragraph is repealed, that is to say, the same Article. Article 154 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 will be applied, this will mean that it will apply, and therefore it will be possible to invite the above-mentioned persons to committee meetings. The third amendment concerns transitional regulation, namely Article. 2: To the meetings of committees and subcommittees convened before the date of entry into force of this resolution, the existing provisions shall apply - if the meetings were ordered earlier than our resolution would have entered into force. Of course, the resolution shall enter into force on the day of its adoption. It is a short proposal to amend the resolution, changes to the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, important. Perhaps there will be a return of such situations that we will have to convene meetings with the possibility of using electronic means for distance communication, so it may be a future solution that will not be used, but, if necessary, it will be possible to reach for them. I would also like to say that the draft resolution was adopted unanimously by the committee, with 15 votes in favour, no one abstained from the committee, no one opposed it. It can therefore be said that the full committee requests these amendments to be adopted in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u323	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to the High House on behalf of the Members of Parliament's applicants the draft amendment to the Act on local taxes and charges, contained in print No 1228. The essence of the amendment is to clarify the provisions contained in Article. Paragraph 7 Point 1 (3) of the Law of 12 January 1991. The current wording is as follows: Buildings, constructions and land seized under real estate tax shall be exempt from the property tax in the area of public airport parts. The general and logical interpretation of this provision is that the airport is a building and is therefore subject to tax exemption. This is due to the definition of construction works under the Construction Law. The building is a building which is not a building or a small architecture, as well as a construction device related to a building which ensures that the building can be used in accordance with its intended purpose. It is worth adding that in accordance with Article. 2 (2) 6 of the Law of 3 July 2002 - Aviation law is an aviation part of an airport which is permanently intended for the take-off and landing of aircraft and the associated movement of aircraft, together with equipment for handling this traffic, to which access is controlled. However, for some time there have been other interpretations of the provisions contained in the article. Paragraph 7 1 point 3 of the Act on Local Taxation and Charges. They aim to recognise that only buildings and buildings and land occupied only for those buildings and buildings in the area of the airport's parts of public use are exempt from real estate tax. This would mean that the remaining land in the area of public airport parts would be subject to tax. The area of this land is significant, often exceeding 100 ha, and therefore the property tax could amount to several hundred thousand PLN, sometimes even more than 1 million PLN. That would be lethal, especially for aeroclubs. I think the High House does not need to be convinced of the importance of aeroclubs: training, sports, education. After all, many young people are drawn to aviation. They're going to aviation right now through aeroclubs. Aeroclubs is also an excellent promotion of Poland. Polish pilots won over 180 medals of the World and European Championships. Sebastian Coffee or Jerzy Makula These are names that are known throughout the sports world. One can only regret that they are often little or less known in Poland. This is a big difference compared to the period of the Second Republic, when the whole nation was fascinated by the successes of Pebble and Wigura, Bajan, Skarżyński. We still have much to do on this issue, but there is no doubt that this activity of aeroclubs is worth supporting, worth highlighting and extremely important for our country. However, it is possible for the aviation world to interpret the rules because of the provision in Article. Paragraph 7 Point 3 (1) is ambiguous. This is why we are proposing a new sound. This entry will be based on the project in this way: Land, buildings and buildings are exempt from real estate tax in the area of public airport parts. Such a provision is similar to the rules on railways, associations or museums and is in line with the intention of the legislator, who granted the facilitation of public airports as having communication functions for the whole of society. It would be highly inappropriate to tax airports while exempting land taken under public road lanes from real estate tax, as enshrined in Article. 2 (2) 3 point 4 of the Act on Local Taxes and Charges, or railway lines, which in turn is enshrined in Article. Paragraph 7 1 point 1 of that Act. The adoption of this amendment of the Act on local taxes and charges will benefit the development of aviation in Poland, will have positive social, economic and financial consequences and will contribute to strengthening the principle of legal certainty. Therefore, I ask the High House to adopt a draft law, which is included in print No 1228. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u325	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! June 25th this year has entered into the Sejm a draft law amending the Act on local taxes and charges. The aim of the project is to clearly indicate that all land, buildings and buildings in the area of public service airports' parts are exempt from property tax, including those land which is not occupied by buildings and buildings located in that area. The draft law concerns the amendment of the article. Paragraph 7 1 point 3 of the Act of 12 January 1991 on local taxes and charges. Art. Paragraph 7 1 reads: Real estate tax is exempt, and here is point 3, buildings, buildings and land seized under them in the area of public airport parts. The wording of this provision so far is ambiguous and can be interpreted as meaning that only buildings and buildings and land occupied only for those buildings and buildings in the area of public airport parts are exempt from property tax. Therefore, it was not explicitly recorded whether or not the other land of the air part of the airport is subject to tax. This was interpreted differently by the municipalities, and the ambiguity of the situation resulted in the case-law of the administrative courts in this respect. The new provision states that, apart from buildings and buildings and the land they occupy, the property tax exemption would cover the remaining land in the area of parts of airports of public use which are not occupied by buildings and buildings located in that area. This is due to the ambiguity of the provision, since the mere fact that civil airports are entered in the register entitles this facility to be regarded as a building. It does not matter whether the land is built or not, because the whole area covered by the airport border is a building. The proposed law therefore aims to ensure that the provisions of the Act clearly and unambiguously state that all land and buildings and buildings in the area of parts of public service airports, including land which are not occupied by buildings and buildings located in that area, are exempt from property tax. Airport rules would therefore be similar to, for example, airport rules. provisions relating to the railways. According to the draft article. 2 The Act proposes that the provisions of this project enter into force on 1 July 2021. I will simply say that the subject matter of the project is not covered by European Union law and its provisions do not require additional implementing acts. Ladies and gentlemen! I would add that there was a contradiction in the provisions between, on the one hand, the Act on local taxes and charges and, on the other hand, the Act on Construction Law. Different definitions in vocabulary as much as in the other act caused all this confusion. This issue needs to be settled. This is merely a clarification, as Dolata's colleague formulated, for the sake of legal clarity. Thank you. Of course, the Law and Justice Club will support the solution proposed by the applicants. ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u327	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Coalition has the honour to present its position on the parliamentary draft law amending the law on local taxes and charges, print No 1228. The Parliamentary bill concerns the extension of the scope of the tax exemption on the property of parts of airports of public use. The current wording of the article. Paragraph 7 Point 1 (3) of the Local Tax and Tax Act indicates that buildings and buildings are exempt from real estate tax and land occupied only by buildings and buildings in the area of public airport parts. This means that other land located in the area of air parts which are not occupied by buildings and buildings in the area is taxed. In this case, the Supreme Administrative Court, by decision of 27 September 2019, clearly identified the scope of the exemptions in this area and pointed out that no exemptions were available when there was no infrastructure at the airports. This is therefore not a clarification of the rules as indicated by the applicant in the explanatory memorandum, but a revision of the rules. The proposed amendment means that the exemption from property tax would be subject to any land, buildings and buildings in the area of public airport parts, including land which is not occupied by buildings and buildings located in that area. I would like to stress that this change does not exclude from tax exemptions buildings in which commercial activities are carried out in any way unrelated to aviation activities. It's worth thinking about, ladies and gentlemen. The draft law will have financial implications for the budgets of local government units. Unfortunately, despite the obligation, applicants did not present the effects of the proposed amendment. I want to warn you that we're not against aeroclubs, just to be clear, because it's not just about aeroclubs. The applicant's explanatory memorandum refers, for example, to exemptions in Article. Paragraph 7 1 point 1 of the Act on local taxes and charges concerning land, buildings and buildings forming part of the railway infrastructure, regardless of the purpose for which they are used. In my opinion, that's not a good argument. This revision of the rules on rail taxation, introduced in 2016, created a tax gap with huge implications for local budgets. There are numerous examples that railway areas are used for highly commercial purposes, in no way related to railway infrastructure, e.g. trade, mines, parking lots, etc., and are exempt entirely from paying property tax. All registration parcels shall be exempt from tax, regardless of the extent to which they are occupied under rail infrastructure. In extreme cases there is even a situation that is less than 1% the area is occupied under railway infrastructure, the remaining 99% under commercial activity and the whole is exempt from real estate tax. The losses for local governments are estimated at several hundred million zlotys per year. In order to clarify the rules on airport property tax, which we do not oppose, it would be necessary to clarify the rules on railway sites in order to eliminate the current tax gap. The relevant draft Senate law on this matter has been in the Sejm since last year and is awaiting consideration. In further work on this project, we will present proposals for amendments to clarify the rules on real estate tax for both airports and railway areas. I am convinced that this proposal will be accepted by the High House. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u331	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Coalition Poland - PSL, Union of European Democrats, Conservatives regarding the parliamentary bill amending the Act on Local Taxes and Charges, print No 1228. This is emphasized by the Polish Districts Association, the Association of Rural Cities of the Republic of Poland or the Association of Polish Cities. Turns out you talked to aeroclubs, but you don't talk to those affected by the changes. The Parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Local Taxation and Charges provides for changes to the rules governing the use of real estate tax exemptions. The Polish Districts Association points out that this change will have a negative impact on the budgets of the municipalities. As pointed out by the association, the solution proposed by the designers will result in all land located in the area of public airport parts benefiting from a real estate tax exemption. Today, however, both in case law and in doctrine, there seems to be no doubt that only land located in the area of parts of airports of public use is covered by the exemption. Therefore, this exemption cannot be used for the land which is located in the area of parts of airports of public use but on which no buildings and structures have been located, as provided for by the judgment of the Supreme Administrative Court of 27 September 2019. The tax rules on land, buildings and buildings at airports, municipal finances, will change. As noted by the Polish Districts Association, after the entry into force of the provision, not only will the exemption not depend on whether the entire area will be in public use in terms of actual operation and universal availability, but also on the land covered by the exemptions will in fact depend on the decision of the President of the Civil Aviation Authority, whose competence it is to approve the boundaries of the air part of the airport. It is also important that the explanatory memorandum does not estimate the financial implications for local government units. In the assessment of the Polish County Association, the justification in this respect is illegible and does not meet the conditions laid down in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm. In a similar tone, the Rural Union of the Republic of Poland stated that the opinion of the Parliament's draft law on amending the law on local taxes and charges, this time relating to aviation infrastructure, is another adverse change in the area of local government finances. It should be pointed out with all determination that this is another planned exemption, which does not provide for compensatory mechanisms. The proposed amendment is not based on editorial conditions or interpretation doubts. This will have a direct negative impact on the budgets of the municipalities. It should be added that any tax exemption is an exception to the principle of universal property taxation. The solutions proposed by the designers will result in land located in the area of public airport parts benefiting from real estate tax exemptions. Such practices will significantly reduce the revenues to the budgets of municipalities. The result of this law will be that tens of hectares of unauthorized real estate will be exempt from real estate tax. The Polish Towns Association also writes about the fact that it is another reduction of the budget, and gives examples. The municipality of Ożarowice, where Katowice-Pyrzowice Airport is located in part, claims that as a result of the new regulations, the municipality which it manages will lose about PLN 6 million a year. Grzegorz Czapla stresses that airport tax revenues are an important part of the municipal budget, and in a municipality without great tourist advantages it will be difficult to replace them. Similar concerns are expressed by the local authorities of Szczytno - airport Olsztyn-Mazury and Świdnik - airport Lublin. I also spoke to the authorities of Szczytno Municipality today. That's $750,000. the influence in the municipality, which does not have much of these influences. If you want to make such exemptions, it must be compensation from the state budget often paid to poor local governments, because you take everything away from them, any influence they may have (the bell), explaining that it is the need for an aeroclub. If there are no such changes proposed, we will not support... Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u333	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! According to the 2002 Act: the aviation part of the airport is an area permanently intended for the take-off and landing of aircraft and the associated movement of aircraft, together with equipment for handling that traffic to which access is controlled. It's an internal operating part of the airport. The airport of public use in turn is: an airport open to all aircraft within the time limits and hours fixed by the airport manager and made public. In 2012, there were very positive changes and new categories of public use airports were introduced, i.e. an aerodrome of public use with limited certification and an aerodrome of public use not subject to certification. The procedure for setting up landing sites has also been liberalised. What did this lead to? This led, ladies and gentlemen, to the creation of nine public service airports with limited certification. These are: Radom-Sadków, Kaniów, Mielec, Białystok, Popobednik, Pleasant, Rudniki, Bednary and Kąkolewo. We also have an additional 25 non-certified public service airports. Nearly 400 new landing sites have also been created. And now that after 2012 these changes have taken place, you can see development, you can see new opportunities, actually it is that those who may have a grudge against the lack of profits from it are self-government. And so now: the law that is proposed actually eliminates the contradictions in the law between the Law - Aviation Law and the Law on local taxes and charges, so that this is a good change. The differences in treatment of different public transport systems, aviation versus road transport, rail transport, as Mr Dolata mentioned, are also eliminated. Preventing the liquidation of 34 local public-use airports that were created after 2012, which themselves are living without public aid, and this is very important, especially for me as a free marketman who claims that such facilities can be maintained alone. This is an area permanently intended for the take-off and landing of aircraft and associated aircraft traffic, including facilities for handling that traffic to which access is controlled. So, ladies and gentlemen, I know that the common understanding of parole... This initial entry in the bill, which is: buildings and buildings, and the land under them, indicates that the airport, especially aeroclub airport, which most often does not have a concrete belt, are two different things. That is: buildings and buildings, and other land. It's just that the rest of the land is a building. The airport is registered in the register of the Civil Aviation Authority and therefore the whole area benefits from the exemption, I will stress once again: it benefits from the exemption because most local governments are asking for it and - here even a few people have talked about it - supports the operation of regional aeroclubs. Now think about it. Some of the respondents paid attention to whether a certain separation should be made when it comes to exemptions, for example. exemptions for aeroclub airports and not for others. Analogical situation is in the case of roads. Public road lanes shall be exempt. We do not distinguish whether it is a communal road, whether it is a district road, a voivodeship, a national road, an express road or a highway. You can say that: well, let's introduce regulations so that municipal and district roads are exempt, and other roads, especially motorways and expressways, are not exempt from real estate tax. This would be an absurd solution. I don't think any of us would sign off on a project like this. In the case of aeroclubs, however, this idea is quite serious. Mr Trela asked directly about the airport in Łódź. He said he figured out that the airport was paying $300,000. PLN property tax per year. The point is that this airport does not pay any zloty property tax per year, because the local government does not charge this tax by interpreting the current provision of the law favorably for the airport manager. The problem is that some interpretations are detrimental to the aviation environment. But most of the local authorities absolutely actually and correctly understand these records, which is a building, which is part of the airport's airfield. This is only because it is not to put airport managers in such a difficult situation who do not know it. There's gonna be municipal elections. The present council, the present mayor, the mayor or the president will interpret this statement properly, i.e. beneficial - and in line with the intention of the legislator - for the airport manager, but after the election there may be some other advice, another mayor, another mayor, who will accept an unfavourable interpretation, i.e. that it belongs to those land which are located in the part of the airport air, but impose a property tax. You will admit that in such a situation, the situation of legal uncertainty is difficult to function. That's why it's a project. He's actually clarifying it. It's not like we're coming up with a new solution. There is no analogy here, because one of your fellow Members referred to railway solutions, layoffs on the railroad. The analogy is that the land, buildings and buildings were written down. You understand that the airport is not the building where it is, I don't know, the airport manager, where there are other hangars, etc. This is an area that allows you to land and take off aircraft. There's just no such analogy. I ask you to take a look at this problem, as it is said, sine ira et studio, calmly. This does not give rise to any financial impact in the vast majority of cases, because local governments, once again I will stress this, interpret this provision in the article. Paragraph 7 1 in such a way that they exempt these airports from tax. In short, they don't charge any taxes for this. Therefore, this amendment will not affect the level of income of these local government units. I'll have another look at the other questions. Will businesses benefit from the exemption? Companies won't benefit from the exemption because companies don't pay that tax. So that's a hypothetical question. Without abuse, it can be said that corruption, so underestimated for 30 years, has contributed significantly to pushing 2.5 million Poles into economic emigration. Within the framework of the contested law, following the guidance contained in the CBA report, we propose an amendment to the Penal Code and six other laws, i.e. the Act on the Implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator, on political parties, on the limitation of the conduct of business by persons performing public functions, on public finances, on sport and on the refund of medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional purposes and medical devices. Corruption isn't just money handed over in an envelope. Corruption is also the bankruptcy of companies that act ethically and are unable to compete with those that are unfairly favored. After all, corruption is also a waste of public resources, which are our common good and should be subject to special protection. Following the solutions introduced in Italy, we propose amendments to the Penal Code that, in addition to the sentence of imprisonment, they should be ruled simultaneously and, as a matter of fact, mandatoryly, prohibit the taking up of positions in public bodies and companies, local government, as well as in companies governed by commercial law, as well as the prohibition of applying for public contracts in order to prevent further ruining of Poland. This ban will be imposed for a period of between 1 and 15 years, which we may consider as a trial period, the first and last warning, for in the case of a redecidive, the ban will be imposed for life. We are aware that these are very strict solutions, but they are necessary to protect the democratic rule of law. In addition, it is proposed to suspend the limitation period for penalties during the period of public functions in order to ensure that sanctions for violations of the law are inevitable. The amendment of the provisions of the Act on the implementation of the mandate of a Member and a Senator bans the execution of the mandate of a Member and of a Senator to work in public institutions. It's the right thing to do. They should also be extended to include employment in companies governed by commercial law, as there is a need to eliminate from the public space a conflict between the public interest and the private interest of parliamentarians. Similarly, it is proposed to introduce a ban on public functions and employment in respect of mayors, mayors or city presidents. We are in favour of transparency and transparency of public life, which is why we propose that political parties keep and keep up-to-date two registers: a register of payments and a register of contracts. Both of these registers will be kept in electronic form and will be kept immediately and no later than 14 days after the date of conclusion of the contract, updated and published in the Public Information Bulletin, so that everyone can get acquainted with them. We propose similar solutions in the revision of the Public Finance Act, which stresses in particular the need to ensure transparency and transparency of public finances through the obligation to publish contract registers. Among the information contained in the register of contracts, it is also proposed to indicate any changes to those contracts. We are convinced that public and publicly available contract registers will contribute to a more efficient management of public funds, which are our common good. In addition, we propose that these anti-corruption solutions apply to the phenomena of corruption in sport, believing that this will contribute to the development of Polish sport and fair competition of athletes. The same is true of the perpetrators of corruption-related crimes as provided for in Article. 7 Act on the refund of medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and medical devices. The example of Italy, where similar solutions have been in place for several years, shows that effective fight against corruption is possible. We want Poland to be a rapidly developing, friendly country for citizens who trust public institutions and people performing public functions. I therefore allow myself to express my hope that all of you will support the proposed anti-corruption rules. I am simply appealing to Members of all groups to actively engage in these proposals so that Poland is a country where citizens will have a real sense of transparency of public life and lack of corruption, as changes in this area have been apparent for 30 years, as reported by both the Polish and the EU. Ladies and gentlemen! We are aware that this is the first step in this direction in order to win against corruption, because, as you have heard, in order for a person to lose his or her job and to be able to apply for public contracts, he or she must be convicted by a final judgment. That is why we hope that soon the judges of peace will be able to relieve the courts and that these very reprehensible corrupt behaviours will be judged in a short time. Of course, another law must be added to the transparency of public life, which would complement this whole package, namely the Anti-Sitva Act, so that people who are employed in public institutions must reveal their affinity, their families, which are in politics. The last element that complements this is a local referendum. The point is that citizens, if they see nepotism or other bad things that happen in communities, can very quickly, easily cancel their uncle, mayor or president. Thank you so much for your attention. The removal of public rights would be decided for a period of between 1 and 10 years, while the prohibition on taking up posts would be imposed for a period of between 1 and 15 years. At the same time, it is assumed that a criminal measure to prohibit the taking of posts will be imposed by the court not only on a mandatory basis, but also on a life-long basis in the event of a repeat conviction of the perpetrator for the offence. The project also prohibits the merger of the work in such companies with the execution of the mandate of Member and Senator and with the performance of the function of governor, mayor or president of the city. However, this provision would not apply to persons who had been given a mandate in Parliament or who were in office in the local government before the entry into force of that law.
