ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u409	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! This project represents an important step in the fight against corruption in Polish public life. Therefore, as a representative of the government, I would like to make a positive point, in detail in writing, but I will refer to the most important points. Firstly, it contains two important objectives. Basically, the idea is to eliminate those convicted of corruption in public life from this public life, and to prevent them from seeking public procurement or operating in public life by means of a clear and strict legal response. Secondly, it is about maintaining clear and transparent principles of public life. This is primarily about financing political parties, which are the primary link of the democratic rule of law, but also of other bodies that have public resources to ensure that the principles are clear and transparent in terms of their financing or contract conclusion. The two objectives of this project are realized. In detail, I would like to point out that the optional deprivation of public rights for corruption and related crimes, such as corruption with sport, with pharmacological measures, with foodstuffs, is very important, mainly because this measure, as far as possible, and therefore optionally adjudicated, responds to the question of the Member, first of all constitutes an appropriate legal response to the wrong done, second of all, preventing the exploitation of profits by the perpetrator convicted of corruption. Secondly, a compulsory criminal measure in the form of a ban on labour in commercial companies, where the Treasury or local government units account for more than 10% Shares or shares are another element in eliminating these black sheep from public life and preventing them from benefiting. This measure will be mandatory. In addition, he will be lifeless in a situation where these people once again commit the crime they committed. Optional measure in the form of a deprivation of public rights and a compulsory measure in the form of a ban on work and a bid for public procurement. In addition, this second objective, which I mentioned, is implemented through a ban on the work of a Member, a governor, a mayor or a city president in all places where there would be a potential conflict of interest. This is a very important solution from the point of view of transparency and transparency in public life. The point is that these people are beyond any suspicion that they cannot be involved in a conflict of interest, even if they are potentially involved. The very important solution that we have not discussed today is to exclude the limitation of corruption offences committed by the mobs, mayors or presidents in connection with and during their duties. As a government, we declare our full support for further parliamentary work. On behalf of the Council of Ministers, I support the bill presented. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u413	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Committee on Culture, which rarely happens, has drawn up a draft resolution by the Sejm on the celebration of the 65th anniversary of the Poznań Uprising - June 1956. The bill was drafted by Mr Rafał Grupiński. I will read you the content of this resolution and I would like to say that I am really proud that the committee in such agreement worked on its content. [[The Gathered Rise Up]] On 28 June 1956, on the streets of Poznań, demanding 'freedom and bread', workers of Poznań's workplaces went out protesting against the terrible conditions of life in the region and in the country. The direct cause of the revolt of the workers was a long-standing conflict in the Zakłady im. Joseph Stalin (formerly and today im. Hippolite Cegielski), employing about 13 thousand. persons. Their crew demanded reimbursement of wrongly charged taxes on withdrawals, improved safety conditions and better organisation of work, which would end the factory's hours of downtime. Employee demands have also been made to raise wages and lower prices, especially food. After the failed mission of the delegation of workers to Warsaw, a general strike began, and then - a manifestation on the streets of the city. The communist authorities to fight the protest, spilled all over Poznań, directed alongside militias and UBs, including the army: 10,000. soldiers and e.g. 359 tanks. The fights on the streets lasted two days and ended with a bloody pacification of the city. At least 70 protesters were killed, more than 600 were injured, more than 700 demonstrators were arrested, nearly 300 were arrested. Persecution lasted long after the days of rebellion. Repression was also given to the defender of the mecs that were arrested. Stanisław Hejmowski. They strongly believed that Poland was under Soviet occupation, and therefore their protest turned into an independent uprising against the power of foreign power. Another reason for the Poznań Uprising - June 1956 was the fact that the communist authorities spent less money on investments in Poznań and Wielkopolska than in other parts of the country, explaining the need to support the most backward areas. The salaries were also lower than in other regions of the country. The Poznań Uprising - June 1956 echoed widely in the country and in the world and was the main beginning of the later partial democratisation of public life in Poland and in other countries of the so-called at the time. Socialist camp. It has also become a reference point for the next workers' protests in Poland and one of the ideal foundations of the universal 'Solidarity' movement, which was born thanks to the democratic opposition and the workers' strikes on the Coast in 1980. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland honors the heroes of the Poznań Uprising - June 1956. Poland will never forget you. Please accept this by acclamation. [[Applause]]

Ladies and gentlemen, there is no way to mention everything in 15 minutes, so I will discuss the most important issues of last year. 2020 is the year of two important anniversaries in our history. The year 1920 - stopping Bolshevism, and 60 years later the rise of the Solidarity, the beginning of the repulsion of the communist system from Poland and from this part of Europe. As in previous years, we wanted to conduct our activities in agreement, in cooperation with many institutions, outside associations. And that's what we did. The year 1920 is not only a great battlefield, but it is a huge commitment of a large part of the conscious nation, which within a few weeks of July and August 1920, when he realized the threat, made a huge effort to create an internal front, mobilization for the voluntary army, mobilization of sanitary services, communications. This effort - a social effort, not a military effort - allowed us to show in the local perspective in 1920. In dozens of places throughout Poland, we unveiled plaques that commemorated volunteers of a well-known area who responded to the appeal of the Council of State Defence, joining the army, offering sacrifices. This allowed us to celebrate the year 1920 with social commitment as much as possible at that time in connection with numerous sanitary restrictions. It is the largest and only scientific project in Poland of the Institute of National Remembrance, which is the "Encyclopedia of Solidarity." The third and fourth volumes appeared in previous years, after a 7-year break, another is in preparation. Thanks to this undertaking thousands of people, often nameless heroes, have found their place in history. The year 1920 is not only the effort of Poles, but also cooperation with other nations. The fundamental rights of a child are the right to life, health care, the right to family education, decent social conditions, the right to study. It's an open directory, but these most important rights are listed. Act II of KK 142/2015 repealed the judgment of the District Court of Sieradz under appeal and referred the case to that court for review. The Supreme Court held that the judgment had been delivered as a result of a gross violation of the procedural law by the District Court of Sieradz by taking into account the prosecutor's request for a fine agreed with the defendants lodged almost one year after the end of the preliminary investigation. ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-24-sejm-33-2.u663	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Today's parliamentary statement I wanted to devote to the harmful phenomenon in the global and European economy, which is tax havens. Despite raising this problem in the European Union by Prime Minister of the Polish Government, Mateusz Morawiecki still thrives. The European Union and its leading countries, such as Germany, France, are trying to impose a moral revolution on Poland through its bodies and are silent about the use of less wealthy taxpayers from Eastern European countries. The phenomenon of tax havens strangles real competition because of privileged companies, leads to illegal financial benefits. By transferring capital to a place of low or zero taxation, corporations and persons charged with a high tax rate avoid the transfer of their own country's debts. They hide the actual level of their income and transfer profits from parent countries, e.g. from Poland, significantly reduce the costs of their actual business. One of the most common opportunities offered by tax havens is the creation of fictional business costs by entrepreneurs. In order to achieve this, company owners form companies based in one of the countries of tax haven and use these fictional branches as a kind of hidden bank. By transferring profits from business to companies registered in a tax paradise, entrepreneurs hide their actual income from tax authorities in their own country. One of the most frequently used methods of artificially reducing profits is the sale of products manufactured by the company to a daughter located in a tax paradise. The products are sold at the manufacturing price, so that the company officially does not make any profits on transactions. The company then sells products at a higher price, thus generating non-taxable profits in the home country and with a favourable tax rate on the spot in the tax paradise. Another popular way that corporations are willing to use is to sell licenses or copyrights to names and logotypes for disproportionately high amounts, making up a large part of all the company's income. Such costs significantly reduce the tax base, which in turn leads to a reduction in the amount of income tax that the company has to pay in its home country. It is estimated that 9 to more than 32 trillion dollars are hidden in tax havens as a result of the use of such runways. According to the Financial Ministry's announcement of 7 July 2020, the EU blacklist of tax havens includes the Republic of Fiji, Guam, Cayman Islands, the Republic of Palau, the Sultanate of Oman, the Republic of Trinidad and Tobago and the American Samoa. This list includes jurisdictions which are not included in the list of countries and territories applying harmful tax competition issued under the Polish income tax legislation PIT and CIT. In addition to these seven jurisdictions, the EU list includes Panama, Samoa, Seychelles, United States Virgin Islands and Vanuatu. A total of 12 dependent countries and territories are listed. Some EU Member States benefit from artificial profit transfer and should be called tax havens. These countries are deliberately implementing such regulations as are conducive to artificial transfer of profits to them. Moreover, these countries are often also used by international corporations as intermediary states to further transfer profits to traditional tax havens such as the Cayman Islands and the Virgin Islands. If the EU does not take effective action against tax havens, it will undermine trust and solidarity between countries. The biggest beneficiaries of tax optimisation are the Netherlands, Ireland, Belgium, Luxembourg, Malta and Cyprus. It's time to put an end to these dishonest practices once and for all.
